    when the customer delivers the security to the selling securities
    broker or to a person designated by the selling securities broker or
    when they cause an acknowledgement to be made to the selling
    securities broker; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the selling securities broker, including a
    correspondence broker, acting for a selling customer fulfils their
    duty to deliver by delivering the security or a like security to the
    buying securities broker or to a person designated by the buying
    securities broker or by affecting clearance of the sale in
    accordance with the rules of the exchange on which the transaction
    took place.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duty to deliver

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Except as provided otherwise in this section and
  unless agreed otherwise, a transferor's duty to deliver a security
  under a contract of purchase is not fulfilled until the transferor
  delivers the security in negotiable form to the purchaser or to a
  person designated by the purchaser, or causes an acknowledgement to be
  made to the purchaser that the security is held for the purchaser.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Delivery to securities broker

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A sale to a securities broker purchasing for the
  securities broker's own account is subject to subsection (2) and not
  subsection (1), unless the sale is made on a stock exchange.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Right to reclaim possession

- **[[233]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-233 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  person against whom the transfer of a security is wrongful may,
  against anyone except a good faith purchaser,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} reclaim possession of the security or obtain
    possession of a new security evidencing all or part of the same
    rights; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} claim damages.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Recovery when unauthorized endorsement

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If the transfer of a security is wrongful by reason
  of an unauthorized endorsement, the owner may reclaim possession of
  the security or a new security even from a good faith purchaser if the
  ineffectiveness of the purported endorsement is asserted against the
  purchaser under section 228.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Right to requisites for registration

- **[[234]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-234
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Unless agreed otherwise, a
  transferor must, on demand, supply a purchaser with proof of the
  transferor's authority to transfer a security or with any other
  requisite that is necessary to obtain registration of the transfer of
  a security, but if the transfer is not for value, it is not necessary
  for the transferor to provide authority to transfer unless the
  purchaser pays the reasonable and necessary costs of the proof and
  transfer.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Rescission of transfer

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If a transferor fails to comply with a demand under
  subsection (1) within a reasonable time, the purchaser may reject or
  rescind the transfer.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Seizure of security

**[[235]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-235 .sectionLabel}** No seizure of a
security or other interest or right evidenced by the security is
effective until the person making the seizure obtains possession of the
security.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 1, s. 235
- 2011, c. 21, s. 97
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-235-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 235"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No liability for acts in good faith

**[[236]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-236 .sectionLabel}** An agent or mandatary,
or a bailee or depositary, who in good faith has received securities and
sold, pledged or delivered them according to the instructions of the
principal or mandator is not in breach of a fiduciary duty or otherwise
liable even though they have no right to dispose of the securities.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 1, s. 236
- 2011, c. 21, s. 97
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-236-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 236"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duty to register transfer

- **[[237]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-237 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If a
  security in registered form is presented for transfer, the issuer must
  register the transfer if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the security is endorsed by an appropriate person;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} reasonable assurance is given that the endorsement
    is genuine and effective;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the issuer has no duty to inquire into adverse
    claims or has discharged that duty;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} all applicable laws relating to the collection of
    taxes have been complied with;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the transfer is rightful or is to a good faith
    purchaser; and

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} any transfer fee referred to in section 180 has
    been paid.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Liability for delay

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} An issuer who has a duty to register a transfer of a
  security is liable to the person presenting it for registration for
  any loss resulting from an unreasonable delay in registration or from
  the failure or refusal to register the transfer.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Assurance of endorsement

- **[[238]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-238 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  issuer may require an assurance that each necessary endorsement on a
  security is genuine and effective by requiring a guarantee of the
  signature of the person endorsing the security and by requiring

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} if the endorsement is by an agent or mandatary,
    reasonable assurance of authority to sign;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if the endorsement is by a fiduciary, evidence of
    appointment or incumbency;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} if there is more than one fiduciary, reasonable
    assurance that all who are required to sign have done so; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} in any other case, assurance that corresponds as
    closely as is feasible to the cases set out in paragraphs (a) to
    (c).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Sufficiency of guarantee

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} For the purpose of subsection (1), a guarantee of the
  signature of a person is sufficient if it is signed by or on behalf of
  a person whom the issuer believes, on reasonable grounds, to be a
  responsible person.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Standards

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} An issuer may adopt reasonable standards to determine
  responsible persons for the purpose of subsection (2).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Sufficiency of evidence of appointment
  or incumbency

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} For the purpose of paragraph (1)(b), the following
  constitute sufficient evidence of appointment or incumbency of a
  fiduciary:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a fiduciary of a deceased security
    holder's estate or succession, a certified copy of the document
    referred to in paragraph 195(1)(c) and dated not earlier than sixty
    days before the day a security is presented for transfer; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the case of any other fiduciary, a copy of a
    document showing the appointment or other evidence believed by the
    issuer to be appropriate.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Standards

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} An issuer may adopt reasonable standards with respect
  to evidence referred to in paragraph (4)(b).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No notice to issuer

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} An issuer is deemed not to have notice of the content
  of a document referred to in subsection (4) that is obtained by the
  issuer except to the extent that the contents relate directly to
  appointment or incumbency.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice from additional documentation

**[[239]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-239 .sectionLabel}** If an issuer, in
relation to a transfer, demands assurance other than an assurance
specified in subsection 238(1) and obtains a copy of a will, trust or
partnership agreement or a by-law or similar document, the issuer is
deemed to have notice of all matters contained in the document that
affect the transfer.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limited duty of inquiry

- **[[240]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-240 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  issuer to whom a security is presented for registration has a duty to
  inquire into adverse claims if the issuer

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} receives written notice of an adverse claim at a
    time and in a manner that provide the issuer with a reasonable
    opportunity to act on it before the issue of a new, re-issued or
    re-registered security and the notice discloses the name and address
    of the claimant, the registered owner and the issue of which the
    security is a part; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} is deemed to have notice of an adverse claim from a
    document that it obtained under section 239.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Discharge of duty

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} An issuer may discharge a duty of inquiry by any
  reasonable means, including notifying an adverse claimant by
  registered mail sent to the address provided by the adverse claimant
  or, if no such address has been provided, to the adverse claimant's
  residence or regular place of business, that a security has been
  presented for registration of transfer by a named person and that the
  transfer will be registered unless, no later than thirty days after
  the date of sending the notice, the issuer

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} is served with a restraining order or other order
    of a court; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} is provided with an indemnity bond sufficient in
    the issuer's judgement to protect the issuer and any transfer agent
    or other agent or mandatary of the issuer from any loss that may be
    incurred by any of them as a result of complying with the adverse
    claim.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Inquiry into adverse claim

**[[241]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-241 .sectionLabel}** (1) Unless an issuer is
deemed to have notice of an adverse claim from a document that is
obtained under section 239 or has received notice of an adverse claim
under subsection 240(1), if a security presented for registration is
endorsed by the appropriate person, the issuer has no duty to inquire
into adverse claims and, in particular,

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} an issuer registering a security in the name of a
  person who is a fiduciary or who is described as a fiduciary is not
  bound to inquire into the existence, extent or correct description of
  the fiduciary relationship and the issuer may then assume without
  inquiry that the newly registered owner continues to be the fiduciary
  until the issuer receives written notice that the fiduciary is no
  longer acting as such with respect to the particular security;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} an issuer registering a transfer on an endorsement by
  a fiduciary has no duty to inquire into whether the transfer is made
  in compliance with the document or with the law of the jurisdiction
  governing the fiduciary relationship; and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} an issuer is deemed not to have notice of the
  contents of a court record or a registered document even if the record
  or document is in the issuer's possession and the transfer is made on
  the endorsement of a fiduciary to the fiduciary specifically or to the
  fiduciary's nominee.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duration of notice of adverse claim

**[[242]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-242 .sectionLabel}** A written notice of
adverse claim received by an issuer is effective for twelve months after
the day it was received unless the notice is renewed in writing.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation on issuer's liability

- **[[243]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-243
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Except as provided otherwise in any
  applicable law relating to the collection of taxes, an issuer is not
  liable to the owner or any other person who incurs a loss as a result
  of the registration of a transfer of a security if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the necessary endorsements were on or with the
    security; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the issuer had no duty to inquire into adverse
    claims or had discharged that duty.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duty of issuer on default

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If an issuer has registered a transfer of a security
  to a person not entitled to it, the issuer must on demand deliver a
  like security to the owner unless

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the issuer is not liable by virtue of subsection
    (1);

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the owner is precluded by subsection 244(1) from
    asserting a claim; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the delivery would result in an overissue to which
    section 196 applies.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Lost or stolen security

- **[[244]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-244 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  owner of a security who fails to notify the issuer of an adverse
  claim, in writing, within a reasonable time after the owner knows of a
  loss, apparent destruction or wrongful taking of the security is
  precluded from asserting against the issuer a claim to a new security
  if the issuer has registered a transfer of the security.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duty to issue new security

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If the owner of a security claims that the security
  has been lost, destroyed or wrongfully taken, the issuer shall issue a
  new security in place of the original security if the owner

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} so requests before the issuer has notice that the
    security has been acquired by a good faith purchaser;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} provides the issuer with a sufficient indemnity
    bond; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} satisfies any other reasonable requirements imposed
    by the issuer.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duty to register transfer

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If, after the issue of a new security under
  subsection (2), a good faith purchaser of the original security
  presents the original security for registration of transfer, the
  issuer shall register the transfer unless registration would result in
  an overissue to which section 196 applies.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Right of issuer to recover

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} In addition to the rights that an issuer has by
  reason of an indemnity bond, the issuer may recover the new security
  issued under subsection (2) from the person to whom it was issued or
  any person taking under that person other than a good faith purchaser.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duty

**[[245]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-245 .sectionLabel}** An authenticating
trustee, transfer agent or other agent or mandatary of an issuer has, in
respect of the issue, registration of transfer and cancellation of a
security of the issuer,

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} a duty to the issuer to exercise good faith and
  reasonable diligence; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the same obligations to the holder or owner of a
  security and the same rights, privileges and immunities as the issuer.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 1, s. 245
- 2011, c. 21, s. 98(F)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-245-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 245"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice to agent or mandatary

**[[246]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-246 .sectionLabel}** Notice to an
authenticating trustee, transfer agent or other agent or mandatary of an
issuer is notice to the issuer in respect of the functions performed by
the agent or mandatary.

## [PART 13]{.HLabel1}[Financial Disclosure]{.HTitleText1} {#h-47356 .Part}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Annual financial statements to members

- **[[247]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-247 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  directors must place before the members at every annual meeting of
  members

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} prescribed comparative financial statements that
    conform to any prescribed requirements and relate separately to

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the period that began on the date the cooperative
      came into existence and ended not more than six months before the
      annual meeting or, if the cooperative has completed a financial
      year, the period that began immediately after the end of the last
      completed financial year and ended not more than six months before
      the annual meeting, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the immediately preceding financial year;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the report of the auditor, if any; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} any further information respecting the financial
    position of the cooperative and the results of its operations
    required by the articles, the by-laws or a unanimous agreement.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Annual financial statements to
  shareholders

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If shareholders have a right to have an annual
  meeting under subsection 133(1), the directors must place the
  documents described in subsection (1) before the shareholders at every
  annual meeting of shareholders.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Despite paragraph (1)(a) and subsection (2), the
  financial statements referred to in subparagraph (1)(a)(ii) may be
  omitted if the reason for the omission is set out in the financial
  statements, or in a note to those statements, to be placed before the
  members at an annual meeting of members and, in a case to which
  subsection (2) applies, the shareholders at an annual meeting of
  shareholders.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 1, s. 247
- 2018, c. 8, s. 69
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-247-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 247"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application for exemption

**[[248]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-248 .sectionLabel}** On the application of a
cooperative, the Director may exempt the cooperative, on any terms that
the Director thinks fit, from any requirement set out in section 247 or
any of sections 249 to 252, if the Director reasonably believes that the
detriment that may be caused to the cooperative by the requirement
outweighs its benefit to the members and shareholders or, in the case of
a distributing cooperative, to the public.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 1, s. 248
- 2018, c. 8, s. 70
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-248-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 248"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Consolidated statements

- **[[249]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-249 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  cooperative must keep at its registered office a copy of the financial
  statements of each of its subsidiaries and of each entity the accounts
  of which are consolidated in its financial statements.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Examination

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The members and shareholders of a cooperative and
  their personal representatives may on request examine the statements
  referred to in subsection (1) during the usual business hours of the
  cooperative and may take extracts from them free of charge.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Barring examination

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A cooperative may, not later than fifteen days after
  a request to examine under subsection (2), apply to a court for an
  order barring the right of any person to so examine, and the court
  may, if it is satisfied that the examination would be detrimental to
  the cooperative or a subsidiary, bar the right and make any further
  order it thinks fit.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} A cooperative must give the person requesting to
  examine under subsection (2) notice of an application under subsection
  (3), and the person may appear and be heard in person or by counsel.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 1, s. 249
- 2001, c. 14, s. 200
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Approval of financial statements

- **[[250]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-250 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  directors must approve the financial statements referred to in section
  247 and the approval is evidenced by the manual signature of one or
  more directors, or a facsimile of the signatures reproduced on the
  statements.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Condition precedent

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A cooperative may not issue, publish or circulate
  copies of the financial statements referred to in section 247 unless
  they are

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} approved and signed in accordance with subsection
    (1); and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} accompanied by the report of the auditor of the
    cooperative, if any.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Copies to shareholders

**[[251]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-251 .sectionLabel}** A cooperative shall
send a copy of the documents referred to in section 247 to each member
and shareholder, except to a member or shareholder who has informed the
cooperative in writing that they do not want a copy of the documents,

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} not less than twenty-one days before each annual
  meeting of members;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} not less than twenty-one days before each annual
  meeting of shareholders, if subsection 247(2) applies; or

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} not later than a resolution in lieu of an annual
  meeting is signed under section 66.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Copies to Director

- **[[252]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-252 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  distributing cooperative, any of the issued securities of which remain
  outstanding and are held by more than one person, must send a copy of
  the documents referred to in section 247 to the Director

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} not less than twenty-one days before each annual
    meeting of members or, without delay after a resolution referred to
    in paragraph 251(c) is signed; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in any event within fifteen months after the last
    preceding annual meeting of shareholders was held or the resolution
    referred to in paragraph 251(c) was signed, but no later than six
    months after the end of the cooperative's preceding financial year.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Subsidiary cooperative

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A subsidiary cooperative is not required to comply
  with this section if its financial statements are consolidated or
  combined with those of its holding cooperative entity and the
  statements of its holding cooperative entity are sent to the Director
  in compliance with this section.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 1, s. 252
- 2001, c. 14, s. 201
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Qualifications of auditor

- **[[253]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-253
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (5), a person
  is disqualified from being an auditor of a cooperative if the person
  is not independent of the cooperative, of any of its affiliates or of
  the directors or officers of the cooperative or its affiliates.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Independence

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of this section,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} independence is a question of fact; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a person is deemed not to be independent if the
    person, or another person with whom the person is in business,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} is a director, officer or employee of the
      cooperative or any of its affiliates, is in business with the
      cooperative or any of its affiliates, or is in business with a
      director, officer or employee of the cooperative or any of its
      affiliates,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} beneficially owns or controls, directly or
      indirectly, a material interest in the securities of the
      cooperative or any of its affiliates, or

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} has been a receiver, receiver-manager,
      sequestrator, liquidator or trustee in bankruptcy of the
      cooperative or any of its affiliates within two years of the
      proposed appointment of the person as auditor of the cooperative.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duty to resign

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} An auditor who becomes disqualified under this
  section must, subject to subsection (5), resign without delay after
  becoming aware of the disqualification.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Disqualification order

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Any interested person may apply to a court for an
  order declaring an auditor to be disqualified under this section and
  the office of auditor to be vacant.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exemption order

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} Any interested person may apply to a court for an
  order exempting an auditor from disqualification under this section
  and the court may, if it is satisfied that an exemption would not
  unfairly prejudice the members or shareholders, make an exemption
  order on any terms that it thinks fit. The order may have
  retrospective effect.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 1, s. 253
- 2011, c. 21, s. 99(E)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-253-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 253"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Appointment of auditor

- **[[254]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-254
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to section 255, the members
  must, by ordinary resolution, at the first annual meeting of members
  and at each subsequent annual meeting, appoint an auditor to hold
  office until the close of the next annual meeting.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Eligibility

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} An auditor appointed under section 82 is eligible for
  appointment under subsection (1).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Incumbent auditor

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Despite subsection (1), if an auditor is not
  appointed at a meeting of members, the incumbent auditor continues in
  office until a successor is appointed.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Remuneration

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The remuneration of an auditor may be fixed by
  ordinary resolution of the members or, if not so fixed, may be fixed
  by the directors.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Dispensing with auditor

- **[[255]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-255 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  cooperative that is not a distributing cooperative may resolve not to
  appoint an auditor by

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} special resolution of the members; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} special resolution of all shareholders, including
    those who do not otherwise have the right to vote.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A resolution under subsection (1) is valid only until
  the next annual meeting of members.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 1, s. 255
- 2001, c. 14, s. 202
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Ceasing to hold office

- **[[256]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-256 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  auditor of a cooperative ceases to hold office when the auditor dies,
  resigns or is removed under section 257.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Effective date of resignation

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A resignation of an auditor becomes effective on the
  date on which a written resignation is sent to the cooperative, or on
  the date specified in the resignation, whichever is later.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Removal of auditor

- **[[257]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-257 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  members may by ordinary resolution at a special meeting remove the
  auditor from office, unless the auditor was appointed by a court under
  section 259.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Vacancy

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A vacancy created by the removal of an auditor may be
  filled at the meeting at which the auditor is removed or, if not so
  filled, may be filled under section 258.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Filling vacancy

- **[[258]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-258
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (3), the
  directors must fill a vacancy in the office of auditor without delay
  after it occurs.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Calling meeting

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If there is not a quorum of directors, the directors
  then in office must, not later than twenty-one days after a vacancy in
  the office of auditor occurs, call a special meeting of members to
  fill the vacancy and, if they fail to call a meeting or if there are
  no directors, the meeting may be called by any member.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Members filling vacancy

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The by-laws of a cooperative may provide that a
  vacancy in the office of auditor may only be filled by vote of the
  members.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Unexpired term

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} An auditor appointed to fill a vacancy holds office
  for the unexpired term of their predecessor.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Court appointed auditor

- **[[259]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-259 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If a
  cooperative does not have an auditor, the court may, on the
  application of a member or shareholder, appoint and fix the
  remuneration of an auditor who holds office until an auditor is
  appointed by the members.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (1) does not apply if a resolution made
  under section 255 not to appoint an auditor is in effect.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Right to attend meeting

- **[[260]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-260 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  auditor of a cooperative is entitled to receive notice of every
  meeting of the cooperative and, at the expense of the cooperative, to
  attend and be heard at the meetings on matters relating to the
  auditor's duties.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duty to attend

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If a director or member of a cooperative, whether or
  not the member is entitled to vote at the meeting, or a shareholder
  that is entitled to vote at that meeting of shareholders, gives
  written notice not less than ten days before a meeting of the
  cooperative to the auditor or a former auditor of the cooperative, the
  auditor or former auditor shall attend the meeting at the expense of
  the cooperative and answer questions relating to the auditor's duties.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice to cooperative

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A director, member or shareholder who sends a notice
  referred to in subsection (2) must send a copy of the notice to the
  cooperative at the same time.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Statement of auditor

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} An auditor is entitled to submit to the cooperative a
  written statement giving the reasons for the auditor's resignation or
  the reasons why the auditor opposes a proposed action or resolution
  set out in any of paragraphs (a) to (d), in any of the following
  situations:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the auditor resigns;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the auditor receives a notice or otherwise learns
    of a meeting of members called for the purpose of removing the
    auditor from office;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the auditor receives a notice or otherwise learns
    of a meeting of directors or members at which another person is to
    be appointed to fill the office of auditor, whether because of the
    resignation or removal of the incumbent auditor or because the term
    of office of the incumbent auditor has expired or is about to
    expire; or

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the auditor receives a notice or otherwise learns
    of a meeting at which a resolution referred to in section 255 is to
    be proposed.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Other statements

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} In the case of a proposed replacement of an auditor,
  whether through removal or at the end of the auditor's term, the
  following rules apply with respect to other statements:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the cooperative must make a statement on the
    reasons for the proposed replacement; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the proposed replacement auditor may make a
    statement in which he or she comments on the reasons referred to in
    paragraph (a).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Circulating statement

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} The cooperative must send a copy of the statements
  referred to in subsections (4) and (5) without delay to every person
  who is entitled to receive notice of a meeting referred to
  subsection (1) and to the Director.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Replacing auditor

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} No person may accept appointment or consent to be
  appointed as auditor of a cooperative if the person is replacing an
  auditor who has resigned or been removed or whose term of office has
  expired or is about to expire until the person has requested and
  received from that auditor a written statement of the circumstances
  and the reasons why, in that auditor's opinion, the auditor is to be
  replaced.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(8)]{.lawlabel} Despite subsection (7), a person otherwise qualified
  may accept appointment or consent to be appointed as auditor of a
  cooperative if, no later than fifteen days after making the request
  referred to in that subsection, the person does not receive a reply.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Effect of non-compliance

  [(9)]{.lawlabel} Unless subsection (8) applies, an appointment as
  auditor of a cooperative of a person who has not complied with
  subsection (7) is null or void.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 1, s. 260
- 2001, c. 14, s. 203(F)
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Examination

- **[[261]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-261 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  auditor of a cooperative must make any examination that is in the
  auditor's opinion necessary to enable the auditor to report in the
  manner that may be prescribed on the financial statements required by
  this Act to be placed before the members or shareholders, except any
  financial statements or parts of statements that relate to the period
  referred to in subparagraph 247(1)(a)(ii).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reliance on other auditor

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Despite section 262, an auditor of a cooperative may
  reasonably rely on the report of an auditor of an entity the accounts
  of which are included in whole or in part in the financial statements
  of the cooperative.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (2) applies whether or not the financial
  statements of the cooperative reported on by the auditor are in
  consolidated form.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Right to information

- **[[262]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-262 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} On
  the demand of an auditor of a cooperative, the present or former
  directors, officers, employees or agents or mandataries of the
  cooperative must provide any information and explanations, and access
  to any documents of the cooperative or any of its subsidiaries that
  are, in the opinion of the auditor, necessary to enable the auditor to
  make the examination and report required under section 261 and that
  they are reasonably able to provide.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Other information

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} On the demand of the auditor of a cooperative, the
  directors must obtain from the present or former directors, officers,
  employees and agents or mandataries of any subsidiary of the
  cooperative the information and explanations that the present or
  former directors, officers, employees and agents or mandataries are
  reasonably able to provide and that are, in the auditor's opinion,
  necessary to enable the auditor to make the examination and report
  required under section 261 and provide them to the auditor.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No civil liability

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A person who in good faith makes an oral or written
  communication under subsection (1) or (2) is not liable in any civil
  action arising from having made the communication.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Audit committee

- **[[263]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-263
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (2), a
  distributing cooperative must, and any other cooperative may, have an
  audit committee composed of not fewer than three directors, a majority
  of whom are not full-time officers or employees of the cooperative or
  any of its affiliates.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exemption

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Director may, on application of a cooperative,
  authorize the cooperative to dispense with an audit committee and the
  Director may, if satisfied that the members and shareholders will not
  be prejudiced, permit the cooperative to dispense with an audit
  committee on any reasonable conditions that the Director thinks fit.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duty of committee

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} An audit committee must review the financial
  statements of the cooperative before they are approved under section
  250.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Auditor's attendance

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The auditor of a cooperative is entitled to receive
  notice of every meeting of the audit committee and, at the expense of
  the cooperative, to attend and be heard at the meeting. If requested
  to do so by a member of the audit committee, the auditor must attend
  every meeting of the committee held during the auditor's term of
  office.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Calling meeting

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The auditor of a cooperative or a member of the audit
  committee may call a meeting of the committee.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice of error

- **[[264]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-264 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  director or officer of a cooperative shall, without delay, notify the
  auditor and the audit committee, if any, of any error or misstatement
  of which the director or officer becomes aware in a financial
  statement that the auditor or a former auditor has reported on.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Errors in financial statements

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If the auditor or former auditor of a cooperative is
  notified or becomes aware of an error or misstatement in a financial
  statement on which the auditor or former auditor has reported, and if
  in their opinion the error or misstatement is material, the auditor or
  former auditor must inform each director accordingly.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duty of directors

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} When under subsection (2) the auditor or former
  auditor informs the directors of an error or misstatement in a
  financial statement, the directors shall

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} prepare and issue revised financial statements; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} otherwise inform the members and shareholders and,
    if the cooperative is one that is required to comply with section
    252, inform the Director of the error or misstatement in the same
    manner as it informs the members.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Qualified privilege

**[[265]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-265 .sectionLabel}** Any oral or written
statement or report made under this Act by the auditor or former auditor
of a cooperative has qualified privilege.

## [PART 14]{.HLabel1}[Trust Indentures]{.HTitleText1} {#h-47530 .Part}

### [Interpretation]{.HTitleText2} {#h-47531 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definitions

**[[266]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-266 .sectionLabel}** The definitions in this
section apply in this Part.

[[event of default]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[event of default]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means, in relation to a
    trust indenture, an event specified in the trust indenture on the
    occurrence of which the principal, interest and other money payable
    under the trust indenture become or may be declared to be payable
    before maturity, but the event is not an event of default until all
    the conditions set out in the trust indenture in connection with the
    giving of notice of the event have been satisfied or the period for
    giving the notice has elapsed. ([cas de défaut]{.DefinedTermLink
    lang="fr"})

[[guarantor]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[guarantor]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a person that has guaranteed
    an obligation of an issuer under a trust
    indenture. ([caution]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[issuer]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[issuer]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a cooperative that has issued,
    is about to issue or is in the process of issuing debt
    obligations. ([émetteur]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[trustee]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[trustee]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a person appointed as trustee,
    including the administrator of the property of others, under the
    terms of a trust indenture to which a cooperative is a party, and
    includes a successor trustee. ([fiduciaire]{.DefinedTermLink
    lang="fr"})

[[trust indenture]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[trust indenture]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a deed, indenture or
    other instrument or act, including a supplement or amendment, made
    by a cooperative under which the cooperative issues debt obligations
    and in which a person is appointed as trustee for the holders of the
    debt obligations issued under it. ([acte de
    fiducie]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 1, s. 266
- 2011, c. 21, s. 100
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-266-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 266"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Application]{.HTitleText2} {#h-47541 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application

- **[[267]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-267 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} This
  Part applies to a trust indenture if the debt obligations issued or to
  be issued under it are part of a distribution to the public.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exemptions

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Director may, in writing, exempt a trust
  indenture from the application of this Part if, in the Director's
  opinion, the trust indenture and the debt obligations under it are
  subject to a law of another jurisdiction that is substantially
  equivalent to the provisions of this Act relating to trust indentures.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 1, s. 267
- 2001, c. 14, s. 204(F)
:::

### [General]{.HTitleText2} {#h-47549 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Conflict of interests

- **[[268]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-268 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} No
  person may be appointed as trustee if there is a material conflict of
  interests between the person's role as trustee and any other role of
  the person.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Eliminating conflict of interests

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A trustee must, no later than ninety days after the
  trustee becomes aware that a material conflict of interests exists,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} eliminate the conflict of interests; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} resign from office.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Validity despite conflict

**[[269]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-269 .sectionLabel}** A trust indenture, any
debt obligations issued under it and the security interest effected by
them are valid even if there is a material conflict of interests of the
trustee.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Removal of trustee

**[[270]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-270 .sectionLabel}** If a trustee is
appointed in contravention of subsection 268(1) or if a trustee
contravenes subsection 268(2), any interested person may apply to a
court for an order that the trustee be replaced, and the court may make
an order on any terms that it thinks fit.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Trustee application

**[[271]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-271 .sectionLabel}** A trustee, or at least
one of the trustees if more than one is appointed, must be a trust
company incorporated under the laws of Canada or a province and
authorized to carry on business as a trustee.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}List of security holders

- **[[272]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-272 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  holder of debt obligations issued under a trust indenture may, on
  payment to the trustee of a reasonable fee and on delivery of a
  statutory declaration to the trustee, require the trustee to provide,
  no later than fifteen days after the delivery to the trustee of the
  statutory declaration, a list setting out the following information,
  as shown on the records maintained by the trustee on the day the
  statutory declaration is delivered to the trustee:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the names and addresses of the registered holders
    of the outstanding debt obligations;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the principal amount of outstanding debt
    obligations of each such holder; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the total principal amount of debt obligations
    outstanding.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duty of issuer

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} On the demand of a trustee, the issuer must provide
  the trustee with the information required to enable the trustee to
  comply with subsection (1).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}When applicant is entity

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If the person requiring the trustee to provide a list
  under subsection (1) is an entity, the statutory declaration required
  under that subsection may be made by a director or an officer of the
  entity or a person acting in a similar capacity.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Contents of statutory declaration

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The statutory declaration must state

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the name and address of the person requiring the
    trustee to provide the list and, if the person is an entity, its
    address for service; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} that the list will not be used except as permitted
    by subsection (5).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Use of list

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} No person shall use a list obtained under this
  section except in connection with

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} an effort to influence the voting of the holders of
    debt obligations;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} an offer to acquire debt obligations; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} any other matter relating to the debt obligations
    or the affairs of the issuer or guarantor of them.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Compliance with trust indentures

- **[[273]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-273 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  issuer or a guarantor of a debt obligation issued or to be issued
  under a trust indenture must provide the trustee with evidence of
  compliance with the conditions in the trust indenture before
  undertaking

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the issue, certification and delivery of debt
    obligations under the trust indenture;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the release, or release and substitution, of
    property subject to a security interest constituted by the trust
    indenture; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the satisfaction and discharge of the trust
    indenture.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Compliance by issuer or guarantor

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} On the demand of a trustee, the issuer or guarantor
  must provide the trustee with evidence of compliance with the
  conditions in the trust indenture by the issuer or guarantor in
  respect of any act to be done by the trustee at the request of the
  issuer or guarantor.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Evidence of compliance

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The following documents constitute evidence of
  compliance for the purposes of subsections (1) and (2):

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a statutory declaration or certificate made by a
    director or an officer of the issuer or guarantor stating that the
    conditions referred to in subsections (1) and (2) have been complied
    with;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} an opinion of legal counsel that the conditions of
    the trust indenture requiring review by legal counsel have been
    complied with, if the trust indenture requires compliance with
    conditions that are subject to review by legal counsel; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} an opinion or report of the auditors of the issuer
    or guarantor, or any other accountant that the trustee selects, that
    the conditions of the trust indenture have been complied with, if
    the trust indenture requires compliance with conditions that are
    subject to review by auditors or accountants.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Further evidence of compliance

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The evidence of compliance described in
  subsection (3) must include a statement by the person giving the
  evidence

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} declaring that the person has read and understands
    the conditions of the trust indenture referred to in subsections (1)
    and (2);

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} describing the nature and scope of the examination
    or investigation on which the person based the certificate,
    statement or opinion; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} declaring that the person has made any examination
    or investigation that the person believes necessary to enable the
    statements to be made or the opinions contained or expressed in the
    statement to be given.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Trustee may require evidence

- **[[274]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-274 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} On
  request of a trustee, the issuer or guarantor must provide the trustee
  with evidence in any form that the trustee requires of compliance with
  any condition relating to any action required or permitted to be taken
  by the issuer or guarantor under the trust indenture.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Certificate of compliance

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} At least once in each twelve month period beginning
  on the date of the trust indenture and at any other time on the demand
  of a trustee, the issuer or guarantor must provide the trustee with a
  certificate stating that the issuer or guarantor has complied with all
  requirements contained in the trust indenture that, if not complied
  with, would, with the giving of notice, lapse of time or otherwise,
  constitute an event of default, or, if there has been failure to so
  comply, giving particulars of the failure.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice of default

**[[275]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-275 .sectionLabel}** A trustee must, no
later than thirty days after the trustee becomes aware of the occurrence
of an event of default, give to the holders of debt obligations issued
under a trust indenture notice of every event of default arising under
the trust indenture and continuing at the time the notice is given,
unless the trustee believes on reasonable grounds that it is in the best
interests of the holders of the debt obligations to withhold the notice
and so informs the issuer and guarantor in writing.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duty

- **[[276]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-276 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} In
  exercising powers and discharging duties, the trustee must

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} act honestly and in good faith with a view to the
    best interests of the holders of the debt obligations issued under
    the trust indenture; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} exercise the care, diligence and skill of a
    reasonably prudent trustee.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reliance on statements

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Despite subsection (1), a trustee is not liable if
  the trustee relies in good faith on statements contained in a
  statutory declaration, certificate, opinion or report that complies
  with this Act or the trust indenture.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No exculpation

**[[277]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-277 .sectionLabel}** No term of a trust
indenture or of an agreement between a trustee and the holders of debt
obligations issued under the indenture or agreement or between the
trustee and the issuer or guarantor operates to relieve a trustee from
the duties imposed on the trustee by this Part.

## [PART 15]{.HLabel1}[Receivers, Receiver-managers and Sequestrators]{.HTitleText1} {#h-47616 .Part}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Functions of receiver or sequestrator

**[[278]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-278 .sectionLabel}** A receiver or
sequestrator of any property of a cooperative may, subject to the rights
of secured creditors, receive the income from the property and pay the
liabilities connected with the property and realize the security
interest of those on behalf of whom the receiver or sequestrator is
appointed, but, except to the extent permitted by a court, the receiver
or sequestrator may not carry on the business of the cooperative.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 1, s. 278
- 2011, c. 21, s. 102
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-278-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 278"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Functions of receiver-manager

**[[279]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-279 .sectionLabel}** A receiver-manager of a
cooperative may carry on any business of the cooperative to protect the
security interest of those on behalf of whom the receiver-manager is
appointed.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 1, s. 279
- 2011, c. 21, s. 102
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-279-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 279"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Directors' powers cease

**[[280]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-280 .sectionLabel}** If a receiver,
receiver-manager or sequestrator is appointed by a court or under an
instrument or act, no director shall exercise the directors' powers that
the receiver, receiver-manager or sequestrator is authorized to exercise
until the receiver, receiver-manager or sequestrator is discharged.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 1, s. 280
- 2011, c. 21, s. 103(E)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-280-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 280"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Appointment by court

- **[[281]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-281 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  receiver, receiver-manager or sequestrator appointed by a court must
  act in accordance with any directions of the court.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Appointment under instrument or act

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A receiver, receiver-manager or sequestrator
  appointed under an instrument or act must act in accordance with the
  instrument or act and any direction that the court may make under
  section 282.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duty

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A receiver, receiver-manager or sequestrator must

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} act honestly and in good faith; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} deal with any property of the cooperative in their
    possession or control in a commercially reasonable manner.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 1, s. 281
- 2011, c. 21, s. 104(E)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-281-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 281"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Directions given by court

**[[282]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-282 .sectionLabel}** A court may, on the
application of a receiver, receiver-manager or sequestrator appointed by
a court or under an instrument or act, or any other interested person,
make any order giving directions on any matter relating to the duties of
the receiver, receiver-manager or sequestrator that it considers
appropriate, including an order

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} appointing, replacing or discharging a receiver,
  receiver-manager or sequestrator and approving their accounts;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} determining the notice to be given to any person or
  dispensing with notice to any person;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} fixing the remuneration of the receiver,
  receiver-manager or sequestrator;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} requiring the receiver, receiver-manager or
  sequestrator, or a person by or on behalf of whom they were appointed,
  to make good any default in connection with their custody or
  management of the property and business of the cooperative, or
  relieving them, or a person by or on behalf of whom they were
  appointed, from any default on any terms that the court considers
  appropriate;

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} confirming any act of the receiver, receiver-manager
  or sequestrator; and

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} giving directions on any other matter relating to the
  duties of the receiver, receiver-manager or sequestrator.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 1, s. 282
- 2011, c. 21, s. 105(E)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-282-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 282"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Required action

**[[283]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-283 .sectionLabel}** A receiver,
receiver-manager or sequestrator must

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} take the property of the cooperative into custody and
  control in accordance with the court order, instrument or act under
  which the receiver, receiver-manager or sequestrator is appointed;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} open and maintain a bank account as receiver,
  receiver-manager or sequestrator of the cooperative for the money of
  the cooperative coming under their control;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} keep detailed accounts of all transactions carried
  out as receiver, receiver-manager or sequestrator;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} keep accounts of the administration as receiver,
  receiver-manager or sequestrator and cause them to be made available
  during usual business hours for inspection by the directors;

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} prepare, at least once in every six month period
  after the day of appointment, financial statements of the
  administration, as far as is feasible, in the form required by section
  247;

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} on completion of their duties, render a final account
  of the administration in the form that the receiver, receiver-manager
  or sequestrator has adopted for preparation of interim accounts under
  paragraph (e); and

- [(g)]{.lawlabel} if section 252 would otherwise apply, file with the
  Director a copy of any financial statement mentioned in paragraph (e)
  and any final account mentioned in paragraph (f) not later than
  fifteen days after it is prepared or rendered.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 1, s. 283
- 2011, c. 21, s. 106(E)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-283-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 283"){.PITLink}
:::

## [PART 16]{.HLabel1}[Fundamental Changes]{.HTitleText1} {#h-47661 .Part}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definition of [[common
share]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}

**[[284]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-284 .sectionLabel}** For the purposes of
this Part, [[common share]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a share in a body
corporate, the rights of the holders of which are equal in all respects,
including equal rights to

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} receive dividends declared by the body corporate on
  the shares; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} receive the remaining property of the body corporate
  on dissolution.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Continuance

- **[[285]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-285 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  body corporate incorporated or continued otherwise than under this Act
  may, if so authorized by its governing legislation, apply to the
  Director for a certificate of continuance under this Act if the body
  corporate

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} satisfies, or by its articles of continuance would
    satisfy, the requirements for incorporation as a cooperative under
    this Act;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} is organized and operated and carries on its
    business on a cooperative basis or, by its articles of continuance,
    causes the body corporate to be organized and operated and to carry
    on its business on a cooperative basis; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} has a capital and corporate structure that, if set
    out in its articles and by-laws, would meet the requirements of this
    Act.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Continuance for the purpose of
  amalgamation

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A body corporate incorporated or continued otherwise
  than under this Act may, if so authorized by its governing
  legislation, apply to the Director for a certificate of continuance
  and a certificate of amalgamation under this Act if the body corporate

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} proposes to be continued under this section for the
    purpose of amalgamating with another body corporate in compliance
    with this Act and does, or will after the amalgamation, satisfy the
    requirements for incorporation as a cooperative under this Act;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} is organized and operated and carries on its
    business on a cooperative basis or, after the amalgamation, will be
    organized and operated and will carry on its business on a
    cooperative basis; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} has a capital and corporate structure --- or after
    the amalgamation will have a capital and corporate structure ---
    that, if set out in its articles and by-laws, would meet the
    requirements of this Act.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Amendments in articles of continuance

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A body corporate that applies for continuance under
  subsection (1) or (2) may, without so stating in its articles of
  continuance, effect by those articles any amendment to its constating
  documents if the amendment is one that a cooperative incorporated
  under this Act may make to its articles.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Articles of continuance

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} If a body corporate wishes to apply for continuance
  under subsection (1), articles of continuance in the form that the
  Director fixes must be sent to the Director, together with any
  information that the Director may require.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Articles of continuance and of
  amalgamation

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} If a body corporate wishes to apply for continuance
  under subsection (2), articles of continuance and articles of
  amalgamation in the form that the Director fixes must be sent to the
  Director, together with any information that the Director may require.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Certificate of continuance

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} The Director must issue

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a certificate of continuance, on receipt of the
    articles of continuance, if the Director is satisfied that the
    requirements for incorporation have been met; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a certificate of continuance and a certificate of
    amalgamation, on receipt of the articles of continuance and the
    articles of amalgamation, if the Director is satisfied that the
    requirements for incorporation and the requirements for amalgamation
    have been met.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reliance on articles

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} For the purpose of subsection (6), the Director may
  rely on the articles.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Effect of certificate

  [(8)]{.lawlabel} On the date shown in the certificate of continuance,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the body corporate becomes a cooperative to which
    this Act applies as if it had been incorporated under this Act;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the articles of continuance are deemed to be the
    articles of incorporation of the continued cooperative; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the certificate of continuance is deemed to be the
    certificate of incorporation of the continued cooperative.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Copy of certificate

  [(9)]{.lawlabel} The Director must send, without delay after the
  certificate of continuance is issued, a copy of that certificate to
  the appropriate official or public body charged with the
  administration of the legislation under which continuance under this
  Act was authorized.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Rights preserved

  [(10)]{.lawlabel} When a body corporate is continued as a cooperative
  under this Act,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the property of the body corporate continues to be
    the property of the cooperative;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the cooperative continues to be liable for the
    obligations of the body corporate;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} an existing cause of action, claim or liability to
    prosecution is unaffected;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} a civil, criminal, administrative, investigative or
    other action or proceeding pending by or against the body corporate
    may be continued to be prosecuted by or against the cooperative; and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} a conviction against, or ruling, order or judgment
    in favour of or against, the body corporate may be enforced by or
    against the cooperative.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Membership shares

  [(11)]{.lawlabel} When a body corporate is continued as a cooperative
  under this Act,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} its common shares are deemed to be membership
    shares to which are attached the rights, privileges and restrictions
    set out in this Act and the articles;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the holders of the common shares of the body
    corporate are deemed to be the members of the cooperative; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} any agreement made before continuance under which
    the holders of any common shares of the body corporate have agreed
    to vote those shares in a manner provided in the agreement is of no
    effect.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Federal credit union

  [(11.1)]{.lawlabel} If a federal credit union, within the meaning of
  section 2 of the [Bank Act](/eng/acts/B-1.01), is continued as a
  cooperative under this Act,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} its membership shares are deemed to be membership
    shares to which are attached the rights, privileges and restrictions
    set out in this Act and the articles;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the members of the federal credit union are deemed
    to be the members of the cooperative; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} any agreement made before continuance under which
    the members of the federal credit union have agreed to vote in a
    manner provided in the agreement is of no effect.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Issued shares

  [(12)]{.lawlabel} Subject to section 182 and subsection (13),

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a share of a body corporate issued before it was
    continued under this Act is deemed to have been issued in compliance
    with this Act and with the provisions of the articles of
    continuance, irrespective of whether the share is fully paid and of
    any designation, right, privilege, restriction or condition set out
    on or referred to in the certificate representing the share;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} continuance under this Act does not deprive a
    holder of any right or privilege that the holder claims under, or
    relieve the holder of any liability in respect of, an issued share;
    and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} shares carry voting rights only to the extent
    permitted by this Act.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Conversion privilege

  [(13)]{.lawlabel} If a cooperative continued under this Act had,
  before it was so continued, issued a share certificate in registered
  form that is convertible to bearer form, the cooperative must not, if
  a holder of such a share certificate exercises the conversion
  privilege attached to the certificate, issue a share certificate in
  bearer form.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definition of
  [[share]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}

  [(14)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of subsections (12) and (13),
  [[share]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} includes a document referred to in any
  of subsections 142(1) to (3), a share warrant within the meaning of
  the [Canada Corporations Act](/eng/acts/C-1.8), chapter C-32 of the
  Revised Statutes of Canada, 1970, or a like instrument.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 1, s. 285
- 2010, c. 12, s. 2113
- 2018, c. 8, s. 72
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-285-20121219.html "Link to previous version of section 285"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Continuance --- other federal acts

- **[[286]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-286 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  cooperative, other than one to which Part 20 or 21 applies, with
  membership share capital may, on special resolution of the members
  and, if the cooperative has issued investment shares, on a separate
  special resolution of the shareholders of each class, apply for
  continuance under the [Canada Business Corporations
  Act](/eng/acts/C-44), the [Bank Act](/eng/acts/B-1.01), the [Insurance
  Companies Act](/eng/acts/I-11.8), the [Trust and Loan Companies
  Act](/eng/acts/T-19.8) or the [Cooperative Credit Associations
  Act](/eng/acts/C-41.01). On the date specified in the document
  evidencing the continuance, that Act applies and this Act ceases to
  apply to the body corporate continued under that Act.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Result of continuance

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} On a continuance under subsection (1), the membership
  shares of a cooperative are deemed to be common shares without a par
  value.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Withdrawal of application

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If a special resolution authorizing the application
  for continuance referred to in subsection (1) so states, the directors
  may, without further approval of the members or shareholders, withdraw
  the application before it is acted on.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Certificate of discontinuance

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} On receipt of a notice satisfactory to the Director
  that the cooperative has been continued under this section, the
  Director must file the notice and issue a certificate of
  discontinuance in the form that the Director fixes.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Date of discontinuance

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} This Act ceases to apply to the cooperative on the
  date shown in the certificate of discontinuance.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Export

- **[[287]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-287
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (6) and Parts
  20 and 21, a cooperative, on a special resolution of the members and,
  if the cooperative has issued investment shares, on a separate special
  resolution of the shareholders of each class, may, if it establishes
  to the satisfaction of the Director that its proposed continuance in
  another jurisdiction would not have an effect set out in any of
  paragraphs (a) to (d), apply to the appropriate official or public
  body of another jurisdiction requesting that the cooperative be
  continued as if it had been incorporated under the laws of that other
  jurisdiction, namely, that the continuance would not

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} adversely affect the members, creditors or
    shareholders;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} result in the cooperative carrying on its business
    and affairs in a manner not consistent with carrying on business on
    a cooperative basis;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} result in a cooperative to which Part 20 applies
    carrying on its business or affairs in a manner not consistent with
    Part 20; or

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} result in a cooperative to which Part 21 applies
    carrying on its business or affairs in a manner not consistent with
    Part 21.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice of meeting

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A notice of a meeting of the cooperative to authorize
  a continuance under this section must be sent to each member and
  shareholder and state that a dissenting member or shareholder is
  entitled to the benefit of section 302, but failure to make that
  statement does not invalidate a discontinuance under this Act.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Abandonment of application

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The directors may, if authorized by the special
  resolution at the time of approving an application for continuance
  under this section, abandon the application without further approval
  of the members or shareholders.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Certificate of discontinuance

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} On receipt of a notice satisfactory to the Director
  that the cooperative has been continued under the laws of another
  jurisdiction, the Director must file the notice and issue a
  certificate of discontinuance in the form that the Director fixes.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Date of discontinuance

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} This Act ceases to apply to the cooperative on the
  date shown in the certificate of discontinuance.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Requirements for continuance

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} A cooperative may not be continued as a body
  corporate under the laws of another jurisdiction unless those laws
  provide that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} its property continues to be the property of the
    body corporate;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the body corporate continues to be liable for the
    obligations of the cooperative;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} an existing cause of action, claim or liability to
    prosecution is unaffected;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} a civil, criminal, administrative, investigative or
    other action or proceeding pending by or against the cooperative may
    be continued to be prosecuted by or against the body corporate; and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} a conviction against, or ruling, order or judgment
    in favour of or against, the cooperative may be enforced by or
    against the body corporate.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 1, s. 287
- 2018, c. 8, s. 73
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-287-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 287"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Shareholders right to vote

**[[288]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-288 .sectionLabel}** Each investment share
carries the right to vote on a continuance under section 286 or 287,
whether or not it otherwise carries the right to vote.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Amendment of articles

- **[[289]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-289
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsections (3) and
  130(2) and sections 134, 290 and 291, the articles of a cooperative
  may be amended by a special resolution to

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} change its name;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} change the place in which its registered office is
    situated;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} add, change or remove a restriction on the business
    or businesses that the cooperative may carry on;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} convert par-value membership shares into
    no-par-value membership shares and provide for a maximum number of
    membership shares that may be issued;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} change a price or formula at which membership
    shares may be issued or redeemed or otherwise acquired by the
    cooperative;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} add, change or remove restrictions on membership;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} convert a cooperative that is incorporated without
    membership shares to a cooperative with membership shares and
    provide for membership shares with a par value and their par value
    or membership shares without a par value and the maximum number of
    membership shares that may be issued;

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} convert a cooperative with membership shares into a
    cooperative without membership shares and provide for the conversion
    of membership shares into member loans;

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} change any maximum number of shares that the
    cooperative is authorized to issue;

  - [(j)]{.lawlabel} reduce or increase its stated capital, if its
    stated capital is set out in the articles;

  - [(k)]{.lawlabel} create investment shares or new classes of
    investment shares;

  - [(l)]{.lawlabel} change the designation of all or any of its
    investment shares and add, change or remove any rights, privileges,
    restrictions and conditions, including rights to accrued dividends,
    in respect of all or any of its investment shares, whether issued or
    unissued;

  - [(m)]{.lawlabel} change the investment shares of any class or
    series, whether issued or unissued, into a different number of
    investment shares of the same class or series or into the same or a
    different number of investment shares of other classes or series;

  - [(n)]{.lawlabel} divide a class of investment shares, whether issued
    or unissued, into series and fix the number of investment shares in
    each series and the rights, privileges, restrictions and conditions
    of them;

  - [(o)]{.lawlabel} authorize the directors to divide any class of
    unissued investment shares into series and fix the number of
    investment shares in each series and the rights, privileges,
    restrictions and conditions of them;

  - [(p)]{.lawlabel} authorize the directors to change the rights,
    privileges, restrictions and conditions attached to unissued
    investment shares of any series;

  - [(q)]{.lawlabel} revoke, diminish or enlarge any authority conferred
    under paragraphs (o) and (p);

  - [(r)]{.lawlabel} increase or decrease the number of directors or the
    minimum or maximum number of directors, subject to section 76 and
    subsection 124(4);

  - [(s)]{.lawlabel} add, change or remove restrictions on the issue,
    transfer or ownership of investment shares; or

  - [(t)]{.lawlabel} add, change or remove any other provision that is
    permitted by this Act to be set out in the articles.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Termination

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The directors may, if authorized by the special
  resolution effecting an amendment under this section, revoke the
  resolution before it is acted on without further approval of the
  members or shareholders.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Restriction on amendment

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If the name of a cooperative is indicative of a
  restriction on the business that may be carried on by it, the articles
  of the cooperative may not be amended to remove that restriction
  unless its name is also amended.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Cooperative basis

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} An amendment to the articles of a cooperative may not
  be made if it would result in the cooperative not being organized or
  operated or not carrying on business on a cooperative basis or, if
  applicable, result in the cooperative not being in compliance with
  Part 20 or 21.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 1, s. 289
- 2001, c. 14, s. 205
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Proposal to amend

- **[[290]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-290
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (2), a person
  referred to in subsection 58(2) or (2.1) may make a proposal to amend
  the articles and section 58 applies, with any modifications that the
  circumstances require, to any meeting of the cooperative at which the
  proposal is to be considered.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice of amendment

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Notice of the meeting of a cooperative at which a
  proposal to amend the articles is to be considered must set out the
  proposed amendment and, if applicable, state that a dissenting member
  or a dissenting shareholder is entitled to the benefit of section 302,
  but failure to make that statement does not invalidate an amendment.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Separate resolutions

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A proposed amendment to the articles referred to in
  subsection (1) is adopted when approved by a special resolution of the
  members and, subject to section 134, if the cooperative has issued
  investment shares, by a separate special resolution of the
  shareholders or the class or series thereof.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Right to vote

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Each investment share that is affected by a proposed
  amendment to the articles carries the right to vote in accordance with
  section 134.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 1, s. 290
- 2001, c. 14, s. 206
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Delivery of articles

- **[[291]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-291
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to any revocation under
  subsection 130(5) or 289(2), after an amendment has been adopted,
  articles of amendment in the form that the Director fixes must be sent
  to the Director, together with any information that the Director may
  require.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reduction of stated capital

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If an amendment effects or requires a reduction of
  stated capital, subsections 151(2) and (5) apply.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 1, s. 291
- 2018, c. 8, s. 74
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-291-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 291"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Certificate of amendment

**[[292]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-292 .sectionLabel}** On receipt of articles
of amendment, the Director must issue a certificate of amendment.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 1, s. 292
- 2018, c. 8, s. 75
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-292-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 292"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Effect of certificate

- **[[293]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-293 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  amendment becomes effective on the date shown in the certificate of
  amendment and the articles are amended accordingly.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Existing claims not affected
